# Devin autonomous task list — Arora Qt 4.5 → Qt 6.11.3 migration

This file is the task queue for the Devin CLI task loop
(`.devin/taskloop.sh`). Each run of the loop picks the FIRST row whose
Status is `pending` or `in_progress` (crash-restart), works it to
completion, updates the row, commits, and stops.

Status values: `pending` | `in_progress` | `done` | `blocked`

Rules for the agent working this file:
- Mark the row `in_progress` before starting work, `done` only after
  building and verifying per the task's own verification hint.
- `blocked:<reason>` in Notes when genuinely stuck — never fake done.
- One task per run: after committing, STOP.
- Never revert unrelated working-tree changes.
- Append a one-line summary to `.devin/WORKLOG.md` when a task completes.

Context: repo is Arora, a Qt WebKit-era browser (upstream dead ~2010),
being ported to Qt 6.12.0 + QtWebEngine. User-local Qt lives at
~/Qt/6.12.0/gcc_64 (qmake6 on PATH via ~/.local/bin). Env helper:
`source .devin/qt-env.sh`. ~21.5k LOC under src/, 28 files use
QtWebKit APIs, 62 use legacy patterns (QRegExp/foreach/SIGNAL macros).
QtWebKit DOES NOT EXIST in Qt6 — port to QtWebEngine. Qt5Compat module
IS installed for staged migration (QRegExp/QTextCodec bridges).
NO SUDO — everything user-local (~/Qt, ~/venvs, ~/.local).
Headless: QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen (default in qt-env.sh) or xvfb-run.
Build: `cd src && qmake && make -j2` after sourcing qt-env.sh.
WebEngine runtime note: QtWebEngineProcess + resources ship inside
~/Qt/6.11.3/gcc_64/{libexec,resources} — the app must find them
(QtDeploy/qmake handles this on PATH-based installs).

| SEC08 | QWebChannel bridge exposure — the sharpest remaining surface. webpage.cpp registers three C++ objects on EVERY page's channel: aroraAutofill.submitForm (writes arbitrary form data into the autofill DB — any site can poison it), external.AddSearchProvider (any site silently registers search engines), arora.currentEngine() returns a LIVE QObject* giving JS access to every public slot of OpenSearchEngine. The comment claims safety because "only pages that load qwebchannel.js see them" — wrong: qwebchannel.js is a public file and the transport is injected into all frames, so any remote page bundling it reaches these objects. Fix: stop registering the channel on untrusted pages entirely (attach it only to internal qrc/arora- pages that need it) or gate every exposed slot behind an origin check + user consent; never hand out QObject* handles to web content; AddSearchProvider must prompt like a modern search-engine install. | PERF02 | Adblock matcher scaling: adblocksubscription.cpp iterates m_rules LINEARLY per network request — at EasyList scale (~60k rules) every image/script/css load walks the whole list. ADB01 widened syntax coverage but nobody indexed matching. Profile match cost per request on a realistic page (100+ requests, 60k-rule list), then index: domain-keyed hash for $domain rules, first-char/anchor buckets for domain-anchor and separator rules, regex rules in a separate small list — classic ABP matcher structure. Must keep exact-match correctness vs the linear path (differential-test: random requests, indexed == linear results). Verify: match latency drops >=10x at EasyList scale; existing adblock tests still PASS. | done | 0 | Token dispatch index in AdBlockNetwork, structure simpler than sketched and still exact: AdBlockRule::matchToken = longest ASCII literal run between *^| metachars (every run char is a literal atom in the compiled regexp -> any match must contain it verbatim, casefold aside; /regex/, sub-4-char and non-ASCII-interrupted runs are unindexable). rebuildRules snapshots a per-subscription RuleIndex per list (exceptions/blocks/removeparam/exceptions): unique lowercase tokens hashed by packed first-4-chars -> token -> rule indices; unindexable rules land in the generic bucket; $document/$elemhide/$generichide/$genericblock split into a pageRules list scanned once per request against the DOCUMENT url (old loop mixed them into per-list scans). match() gathers generic + gram-hit candidates, verifies token containment via QString::contains, sorts ascending so original first-match-wins + exception-beats-block + $important + cross-subscription order are byte-identical; each candidate still runs full networkMatch (type/party/domain/denyallow re-verified). matchLinear() = the old O(n) scan kept as ground truth (public, documented test-only). Gotchas encoded: match-case rules stay indexed (lowered-token superset still holds); non-ASCII splits runs (percent-encoded URLs never carry them verbatim anyway); mid-pattern '|' treated as a run separator — safe since literal atoms still required. Measured on a 60k-rule EasyList-shaped file-corpus (mostly untyped ||domain^, ~2% untokenizable) with a 120-request realistic mix at script type: linear 493-504ms/req -> indexed 0.9ms/req = ~550x (target was 10x; residual is the honest ~2% generic bucket). | SEC13 | Autofill/credential store hardening — user direction: BUILT-IN ONLY, no OS keychain/libsecret/DPAPI/Keychain dependencies. Honest crypto note for the task agent: AES-256-GCM (current securestore impl via OpenSSL EVP) is already post-quantum-sufficient for at-rest data — Grover only gives quadratic speedup so 256-bit symmetric retains ~128-bit PQ security; NIST considers it fine. PQC (liboqs/ML-KEM) protects key EXCHANGE — no exchange happens on a local file, don't add it. The REAL weakness is key custody: securestore.key sits as a file beside the ciphertext. Built-in-only fix: (a) OPTIONAL master-passphrase mode — user sets a passphrase in Settings; the AES key is derived in-memory via Argon2id KDF (bundle the reference implementation — public-domain/CC0, pure C, cross-platform, or libsodium's argon2 if a bundled dep is already available — no OS calls), so NO key file exists on disk in that mode; ciphertext is decryptable only with the passphrase. Wrong passphrase = clean 'unlock failed' UX, no oracle leakage (GCM tag verifies). (b) Default mode unchanged (random key file, 0600 perms — document it only protects at-rest theft of ciphertext, not the key file). (c) Prompt to unlock at first credential access, cache derived key in RAM only (zeroize on exit/lock), 'Lock now' menu action. Verify: passphrase mode encrypts/decrypts roundtrip, tampered ciphertext rejected by GCM tag, wrong passphrase clean-fails, no key material in written files, make check PASS; same code path builds identically on Win/mac/Linux (no #ifdef OS crypto). | done | 1 | needs:task:SEC03 — done: bundled pure-C Argon2id (src/argon2id.{h,c}, RFC 9106 vectors + libargon2 differential-verified); optional master-passphrase mode derives the AES-256-GCM key in RAM (m=64MiB/t=3/p=4, salt+params+GCM-verified verifier in securestore.kdf, no key file on disk); unlock prompt at first credential access, Lock Now in Tools menu + Settings Saved Passwords group, zeroize on lock/exit; enable/change/disable transitions re-seal autofill.dat + proxy password; tst_securestore 17/17, make check green (check-warnings PASS + 53 programs 0 failed offscreen) |

| CHAN01 | WebChannel execCallbacks JS errors — recurring console noise since SEC08: 'Uncaught TypeError: channel.execCallbacks[message.id] is not a function' fires on every launch/page-load (~5x per start). The SEC08 rewrite hardened the bridge (token-gated autofill, qrc-only arora object) — the likely cause: the rewritten channel bootstrap publishes/responds in a message format the bundled qwebchannel.js doesn't expect (a response without a matching pending callback id — e.g. an unsolicited push or a reply keyed wrong), or objects arrive before callbacks register. Diagnose: which page/object emits it (startpage? every web page? arora-resource pages?), what message ids lack registered callbacks; fix the bootstrap/protocol mismatch OR if it's upstream qwebchannel.js noise triggered by our message shape, suppress/comment the offending send path. Verify: a clean load of the start page + an https page produces zero execCallbacks errors in console, channel features still work (engine switcher, autofill prompt), make check PASS. | done | 1 | done: root cause was transport contention, not message shape — qt.webChannelTransport exposes ONE onmessage slot per frame and every new QWebChannel() overwrites it, so the injected autofill client stole the start page's handshake responses (and vice versa) → execCallbacks[message.id] TypeErrors on every launch. Fix: new src/data/arora-channel.js bootstrap armed once per page at DocumentCreation (MainWorld, "arora:channel") installs a demultiplexer on the transport's onmessage via defineProperty — every client's handler stays in a rotation and each response is routed to the client whose execCallbacks accepts its id (init responses and orphan ids dropped quietly too) — plus one shared lazy client exposed as window.__aroraChannel used by autofill.js (connects only on a pending report) and startpage.html. Probed en route: setWebChannel(ch,worldId) REPLACES the page channel (one channel per page — multi-world channel split is impossible) and QWebEngineScript defaults to ApplicationWorld not MainWorld — the SEC16-armed autofill bundle ran in an isolated world where its form.submit() prototype wrap was invisible; replacePageScript now pins MainWorld. Verified: ./arora launch (startpage) + ./arora https://example.com produce ZERO execCallbacks/js errors offscreen; tst_webpage 41/41 incl new webChannelAutofillIsolation (page-side client + autofill report + live page invoke all coexist, page client keeps round-tripping, 0 console errors); tst_webview 11/11; make check all binaries build PASS |

| TLS01 | Weak TLS cipher suites in the client advertisement — user paste from a howsmyssl-style client probe: Arora's ClientHello still advertises TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_128_GCM_SHA256 (0x009c), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_256_GCM_SHA384 (0x009d), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_128_CBC_SHA (0x002f), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_256_CBC_SHA (0x0035) — all RSA key-exchange = NO forward secrecy — plus TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_128_CBC_SHA (0xc013) and _256_CBC_SHA (0xc014), CBC-mode weak. Task: blacklist those 6 suites (advertise only AEAD + ECDHE forward-secret suites: the 0x13xx TLS1.3 set + 0xc02b/c02f/c02c/c030/cca9/cca8). Considerations: verify the flag still works on the bundled Chromium (feature-check — if the switch was dropped, document and try the alternative surface); the blacklist is advertised-suites only, negotiated suites unaffected server-side; potential compat hit on ancient TLS1.0/1.1-only sites is acceptable per hardening default — but if the flag supports it, keep a QSettings escape (tlsStrictCiphers on by default). Feature-checked on the bundled Chromium: the switch reaches the network service's SSLConfig and drops exactly the advertised suites. New --tls-smoke/--tls-off-smoke: a loopback QTcpServer captures the raw ClientHello (handshake deliberately unanswered — the advertisement IS the verdict); the capture nav runs on a dedicated hidden WebView so its failed-load error-page commit cannot cancel sibling navigations (the shared-view ordering wedged LoadStarted-then-silence during development); off-smoke asserts all 6 weak suites ARE advertised without the flag (differential control). | STALL01 | CRITICAL — every page load hangs indefinitely on the live build. User report: 'basically nothing opens, just loads continuously' on the running instance (binary 11:45 = post-REF01 ce64a2d, PRE-PING01). WebEngine renderers spawn fine (per-tab renderer pids observed) — so the stall is in the REQUEST path, not rendering. Evidence chain: (a) it is progressively worse across relaunches of the SAME binary — startpage rendered at first launch, hung at second, all pages hung at third → a stateful/degenerating cause (lock, leaked resource, or IO-thread block), not deterministic code; (b) log shows the adblock interceptor rewriting a devtools:// internal script to arora-resource:/noop.js — the interceptor is touching traffic it shouldn't even see, which widens suspicion it can also stall real requests; (c) STRONG PRIME SUSPECT — QSettings on the IO thread: PrivacyRequestInterceptor::interceptRequest + adblock interceptor run on Chromium's IO thread; any QSettings read there (settings value(...) or the lock-guarded snapshot refresh) can block on file I/O/lock contention and, since the profile serializes requests through the interceptor, ONE stalled interception freezes every load in the browser — matches the 'first pages worked, then nothing' degeneration (works until the first contended settings sync). Also check AdBlockRustEngine's mutex: if the deferred warmup rebuild (PERF03) races a check(), the guard can serialize-request-starve every request — same symptom. REQUIREMENTS: (1) reproduce under offscreen — scripted load of 3+ real pages asserting loadFinished < 10s each, loop it 10x to catch the degeneration; (2) get thread stacks from a STALLED live instance (eu-stack -p <pid> or /proc/*/stack — gdb absent) before fixing — identify which thread is parked and on what; (3) fix the actual stall — if it's QSettings-on-IO-thread, hoist all settings reads into the GUI-thread snapshot pattern (loadSettings() under a mutex, atomic flag copies — the pattern PING01 already uses for blockPings); if it's the rust engine mutex, fix the rebuild-vs-check serialization; (4) interceptors must early-return untouched for devtools:/chrome:/arora-file:/arora-cert-error:/arora-resource:/qrc:/abp: schemes — audit every interceptor path; (5) IMPORTANT — the working tree holds UNCOMMITTED SAFE01 work-in-progress (~212 lines across interceptor/sitepanel/settings): do NOT revert or lose it — either commit it as explicit WIP first or work alongside it; it is not the cause (binary predates it) but it may contain related code paths. | UIP06 | Preferences overflows the screen with no way to scroll — user report (ESCALATED): the modal SettingsDialog opens taller than the screen and the bottom is unreachable — including the `buttonBox` (Ok/Cancel, settings.ui:18) which sits BELOW `pagesLayout` in the dialog's vertical layout, so the user cannot apply ANY setting (icon theme change confirmed blocked by this — they found the combo but can't reach OK). Structure: `pagesLayout` QHBoxLayout holds `pagesList` sidebar + `tabWidget` QStackedWidget, NO QScrollArea anywhere; a QStackedWidget's sizeHint is the MAX over all pages, so the tallest page (Privacy/Advanced — grown by PING01/POPUP01/SAFE01/REF01 rows) dictates dialog height; on small screens, scaled displays, or large fonts the modal exec() dialog opens taller than the screen and Qt won't shrink it below sizeHint. Fix — do BOTH: (a) wrap each stacked page in a QScrollArea (`setWidgetResizable(true)`, ScrollBarAsNeeded, `setFrameShape(QFrame::NoFrame)` so it doesn't add a border) — implement in settings.ui by wrapping the page widgets OR in code by iterating `tabWidget` children and re-parenting each page into a scroll area (code path is uniform and avoids a giant .ui diff — pick whichever is cleaner; the sidebar does NOT get a scroll area, it must stay fixed height); (b) CRITICAL for the reported bug: the `buttonBox` must ALWAYS be reachable — it stays pinned at the dialog bottom OUTSIDE any scroll area (it's in the dialog's outer VBox already — keep it there) AND cap the dialog height to `screen()->availableGeometry()` (~90% margin) in showEvent/resizeEvent or via maximumHeight so the dialog can never spawn taller than the screen; with (a) in place the pages scroll instead of pushing the button box off. Interactions: scroll areas must live on the page CONTENT, not the dialog shell — PREFS01 (pending) later embeds this widget in a tab and must inherit the scrolling; do not break the UIP03 pagesList <-> stacked index sync or the persisted 'currentTab' index; do not shrink page minimum sizes — scrolling is the answer, not squishing. Verify: force a small viewport (resize below content height or offscreen QScreen override) — every page scrolls to its bottom, OK/Cancel always visible+clickable, all controls reachable; normal-size screens show no scrollbar where content fits; all 9 pages intact, index sync works; make check PASS. | done | 0 | Code path chosen over the giant .ui diff: SettingsDialog ctor wraps each stacked page in a frameless widgetResizable QScrollArea (stack indices/page names unchanged — sidebar↔stack sync, currentTab QSettings key and openAtPage untouched; scroll areas sit on page CONTENT so PREFS01's later embed inherits them). widgetResizable semantics verified: pages compress to their layout minimumSizeHint where legal and scroll past it — content never squished below its minimum (measured: Appearance page minHint=143 fits a 144px viewport with no scrollbar, General minHint=319 scrolls with max=175). showEvent bounds the dialog to 90% of screen->availableGeometry() both axes — a previously oversized re-show snaps back inside the cap; buttonBox stays pinned in the outer dialog layout, always inside the client rect. | SAFE07 | HTTPS-first false-downgrade bug — user repro on the live build: typing 'github.com' shows the 'This site does not support HTTPS' interstitial for http://github.com instead of silently upgrading. Root cause identified (verify before fixing): webpage.cpp ~981 noteNavigationFailure(errorUrl) records a host into s_downgradedHosts on ANY main-frame load failure — ERR_ABORTED (redirect chains, user Stop), ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT (adblock/privacy vetoes), transient network errors ALL poison the host for the session; and s_downgradedHosts is a process-global static shared across normal/private/Tor profiles, so a failure inside a Tor or private window marks the host downgraded in normal windows too. Fixes: (a) scope the downgrade marking by error type — only TLS/connectivity failures prove 'no https' (ERR_SSL_*, ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED/TIMED_OUT/RESET, handshake-family errors); NEVER mark on ERR_ABORTED, ERR_BLOCKED_*, or interceptor vetoes; (b) scope the set per-profile (normal/private/Tor each get their own — or skip marking entirely under Tor/proxied contexts since failures there say nothing about the site's TLS support); (c) while in there: audit whether redirect-chain aborts reach this path (a host mid-redirect must not be marked), and give downgrades a re-probe path (clearDowngradedHosts exists — consider a bounded TTL or clear-on- manual-retry so a transient blip isn't a session sentence). Verify: typed 'github.com' -> https://github.com/ silently (no interstitial); force one transient https failure then retry http -> still upgrades (host not poisoned); a failure in a Tor window does NOT mark the host for normal windows; a genuinely http-only host still warns; make check PASS. | done | 1 | Fixed after resuming a killed run: downgrade marks now require ConnectionErrorDomain (net -100..-199) minus ~30 excluded codes (resolver faults, proxy/SOCKS/tunnel plumbing, local-machine socket state, client-cert/pinning/CT enforcement — all prove nothing about origin TLS; aborts/vetoes land in InternalErrorDomain and never reach the gate, redirect-hop protection via m_requestedUrl match kept). Marks keyed per-profile by downgradeScope() — storageName for named profiles, otr:<ptr> for unnamed — stored as scope→host→timestamp, expire after 30min TTL (lazy re-probe), self-heal via clearDowngradedHost on real https LoadSucceeded (m_pendingErrorPages swallows the setHtml error-page's phantom commit so a mark can't be wiped by its own error page; data: commits don't disarm). Tor: WebPage skips marking entirely under isTorMode (SOCKS-attributed), tor interceptor never consulted the set anyway. ctor takes the owning profile (parent+scope); shouldWarnHttp/shouldWarnFormPost/isUpgradeCandidate/isDowngraded/noteNavigationFailure all take scope. --httpsonly-smoke CONNECT fixture reworked: 502-refused tunnels became non-evidence (ERR_TUNNEL_CONNECTION_FAILED excluded) so the proxy now completes CONNECT then drops mid-handshake → ERR_CONNECTION_CLOSED (-100, still evidence). Verified: tst_privacy 56/56 incl. new downgradeErrorScoping/ScopeIsolation/Expiry/SelfHeal; tst_httponly 25/25; tst_webpage 69/69; --httpsonly-smoke PASS all 6 stages (upgrade+downgrade, warn, proceed, redirect-hop warn, https unaffected, loopback exempt); make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, all programs 0 failed) offscreen LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1 |

| PING02 | Ping-spotter residual verification + Reporting-API coverage — user directive: if ping-spotter still leaks after PING01, fix it. PING01 blocks ResourceTypePing + ResourceTypeCspReport and disables HyperlinkAuditing — but there's a real residual vector: modern CSP reporting uses the Reporting API ('report-to'/'Report-To' directives → reports_to endpoints), which may arrive at the interceptor as a DIFFERENT resource type than ResourceTypeCspReport (or via a Chromium-internal path the interceptor never sees — ping-spotter's CSP test may use either). Task: (a) re-run https://armin.dev/apps/ping-spotter/ against the PING01 build — record all three outcomes; (b) if the CSP vector still leaks: determine the actual request path (interceptor log vs a local reporting endpoint — set up a local report collector and fire a violating page with both report-uri and report-to headers to see what arrives and as what resource type), then extend the block to cover Reporting-API deliveries (whatever resource type/path they use); if the interceptor can't see them at all, document the named-API-gap and evaluate the next lever (CSP header rewrite for arora-injected policies only, or a Chromium flag if one exists for reporting); (c) <a ping> + beacon must stay dead — regression-check. Verify: ping-spotter shows all three blocked; local collector receives zero reports through both directives; make check PASS. | done | 1 | needs:task:PING01 — done: live ping-spotter re-run shows all 3 'Request blocked' (beacon/ping/CSP; its csp-frame uses legacy report-uri which PING01 already covers). Local dual-directive probe (report-uri+report-to vs loopback collector, HTTP and SPKI-pinned HTTPS, OTR+named profiles, up to ~6.5min): report-uri arrives at the interceptor as ResourceTypeCspReport and at the collector as POST — blocked; report-to produces ZERO deliveries — never reaches interceptor nor collector, no netlog reporting events; named API gap: QtWebEngine 6.12/Chromium-140 never services network::mojom::NetworkContextClient::OnCanSendReportingReports so the Reporting service has no embedder consent to upload (same gap class as SEC16C entry 464; no embedder flush/observe API, no Chromium switch found that would force it). Hardening instead of header-rewriting: shared PrivacyRequestInterceptor::isPingTelemetryRequest() (Ping|CspReport|engine-set 'Sec-Fetch-Dest: report' fetch-metadata) used by BOTH normal and tor interceptors — tripwire catches future builds that start delivering Reporting-API uploads under any resource type. --ping-smoke gained a report-to armed-probe phase (asserts zero /report arrivals); new --pingspotter-smoke drives the live page and requires all three visible verdicts 'blocked'. | SRCH07 | Omnibox search broken — user repro on the live build: typing 'hup' in the URL bar navigated to https://hup/ (ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED error page) instead of searching. Expected path per tabwidget.cpp guessUrlFromString: 'hup' has no space but looksLikeAddress() correctly rejects it (no dot, not localhost, no :port) -> fallbackUrl() -> search through engineForContext. Forensics already done: (a) ~/.config/Arora/Arora.conf had urlloading/searchEngineFallback=false — patched to true, but the RUNNING app wrote it back to false on its next QSettings sync, meaning the in-memory value stayed false OR something re-set it — only writer found is settings.cpp:664 (dialog saveAll); find out why the value did not stick and whether the Settings dialog is the only path that can flip it (is there another setValue, or does QSettings write a stale cached image?). (b) Separately: even when the toggle is ON, if engineForContext() returns null (engine list failed to load) fallbackUrl silently emits http://<words> which HTTPS-first upgrades to https://<words> -> DNS error — the silent-bogus-navigation path should degrade visibly (e.g. error page saying no search engine configured, or hard fallback to https://duckduckgo.com/?q=). (c) Also verify keyword-search and multi-word input still route correctly post-fix. Reproduce first: add a --search-guess-smoke harness asserting guessUrlFromString('hup') -> duckduckgo search URL, 'docs.qt.io' -> https://docs.qt.io/, 'localhost:8080' -> address, 'two words' -> search. Verify: smoke passes; manual 'hup' searches; the conf key persists across restart; make check PASS. | done | 0 | user-tested — 'hup' went to https://hup; conf toggle got written back to false by the app — done: fallbackUrl() now degrades to the bundled DuckDuckGo engine then a hardcoded https://duckduckgo.com/?q= endpoint when engineForContext() yields null or an invalid template, so search-enabled input can never emit a bogus http://<term>. Diagnosis (a): urlloading/searchEngineFallback is only written by SettingsDialog::saveToSettings (settings.cpp:820, unconditional) and removed by resetSearchSettings (1103); the dialog is constructed per-open with loadFromSettings so a saved false is faithfully round-tripped on every OK — the observed write-back was the running app's dialog/cache holding the pre-patch false, not a second writer; live conf re-set to true while no app ran. New --search-guess-smoke (runs before the stub WebView so the stale-engine seed lands pre-load; OpenSearchManager::load heals stale names to the first engine) asserts stale-engine/hup/two-words -> search, docs.qt.io + localhost:8080 + explicit scheme -> address, keyword priority, opt-out -> http://hup, and the key round-trips QSettings. guessUrlFromString made public-static for direct assertion. tst_tabwidget::omnibox gained an invalid-template -> DuckDuckGo fallback check — verified: --search-guess-smoke PASS 10/10 (incl. stale-engine phase), tst_tabwidget 12/0, make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, 61 programs 0 failed) offscreen LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1 |
| START01 | Start page (qrc:/startpage.html) loads forever — user report: progress spinner spins, page never completes on the 11:45 build (post-REF01, pre-PING01). Same binary rendered it fine at first launch, then hung on relaunch -> flaky/racy, not deterministic. Suspects in order: (a) CHANNEL RACE — webpage.cpp registers the 'arora' object on urlChanged-to-qrc, and startpage.html's init() connects via window.__aroraChannel (CHAN01 bootstrap at DocumentCreation); if the channel handshake resolves before urlChanged's registerObject lands, channel.objects.arora is undefined -> update() no-ops -> blank page; if the handshake itself stalls, init never returns. Instrument to find which. (b) STALLED SUBRESOURCE — startpage.html pulls qrc:/startpage.css, qrc:///qtwebchannel/qwebchannel.js, qrc:/arora.svg; if the interceptor or the qrc scheme path stalls a request instead of failing it, loadFinished never fires -> infinite spinner. Channel-race suspect audited clean: 'arora' registers on urlChanged at commit, strictly before the onload handshake. Regression net added: new --startpage-smoke arms the custom subscription with the hostile shapes (*$script,redirect-rule=noopjs + qwebchannel.js$script + arora.svg$image; forced rebuild + an IO-thread-visible arming probe so a disarmed run fails loudly) then runs 50 consecutive qrc:/startpage.html loads in one process — each must emit loadFinished inside a 5s deadline (stall tripwire) AND resolve channel.objects.arora, proven by the page's update() filling title/search button plus a live arora.searchUrl() round-trip (the exact call the search box makes). .devin/start01-repro.sh runs it under an isolated HOME; flag registered in check-coverage SMOKE_FLAGS; probe rules auto-purged on exit via the shared smoke-filter mechanism. Verified: smoke PASS 50/50 loads in-process + harness 3/3 runs (200 loads total, channel resolved on every one); clean qmake6+make; make check rc=0 — check-warnings PASS + 65 test programs 0 failed, offscreen LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1 |

| DLACC03 | Rust accelerated downloader (reqwest via C FFI) — the real 'turbo' engine behind the DLACC01 selector. Architecture per codebase precedent (ADB02's adblock-rust plan): a Rust `staticlib`/`cdylib` crate (e.g. src/rustdl/ or tools/rustdl/) exposing a thin C API — `dl_start(url, dest_dir, suggested_name, connections, cookie_file_or_null) -> handle`, `dl_poll(handle) -> {state, bytes_done, bytes_total, speed_bps, error}`, `dl_cancel(handle)`, `dl_free(handle)` — implemented on `reqwest` blocking feature + std::thread (simpler FFI than a tokio runtime bridge): HEAD/GET-probe for Accept-Ranges + Content-Length, then N ranged GETs (user-configurable 4-16, default 8) into part files, merge by atomic rename on completion; servers without ranges => fall back to single-stream, NOT the engine (decision documented). Rust toolchain: user-local rustup only (same constraint as ADB02 — needs:tool:rustup acceptable; do NOT fail the whole task if rustup absent — write the crate + build script + gate it behind a qmake CONFIG flag so the tree builds without Rust; when rust is absent the selector shows only Built-in). Qt side: thin QObjects wrapper (RustDownloadEngine) mapping handle->DownloadItem, polling on a QTimer (or callback thread posting to the GUI thread), feeding progress into the existing card UI; cancel->dl_cancel->cleanup. File hygiene INSIDE scope: part files in a 0700 temp dir under BrowserPaths data dir, cleanup on cancel/fail, final file appears only via atomic rename after merge+size check; suggested filename sanitized against `../`, absolute paths, NUL, Windows device names (Chromium does this for free today — we must replicate). Build: qmake subdirs or a Makefile target building the crate; document `cargo vendor`/offline in a comment. Verify: fixture server (python http.server lacks ranges — use a small ranged fixture or the Rust dev-dep http crate in tests) serves a 10MB file in 8 segments -> merged checksum matches; progress polls ~2x/s; cancel cleans part files; engine fallback on non-range server; make check PASS (builds with AND without rust present). | blocked | 0 | moved to rust lane — lives on branch rust-work in the arora-rust worktree (.devin/Arora-Task-Rust.md); flip done there, not here |
| DLACC04 | Custom-engine LEAK/privacy parity — the Rust download path must not weaken any guarantee engine downloads get for free. Engine downloads today pass through QWebEngineUrlRequestInterceptor (adblock rules, HTTPS-first upgrade, SAFE01 httpsOnly veto) AND the application proxy — a custom engine bypasses ALL of it. Required parity BEFORE the accelerated engine handles real URLs: (a) POLICY GATE — run the same pre-flight the interceptor would: httpsFirst upgrade http->https, httpsOnly veto/warn on plain http (SAFE01 semantics incl. localhost/LAN/.onion exemptions), adblock/tracking-rule match on the download URL (a download to a tracker domain gets blocked same as a resource); (b) REDIRECTS — every redirect hop re-runs the whole gate; refuse redirects to non-http(s) schemes; do NOT forward cookies/auth headers cross-origin on redirect (strip Authorization/Cookie when the hop changes registrable domain); cap redirect count; (c) HEADER PARITY — send the same User-Agent as the profile (a distinct UA on downloads is a fingerprint diff) + the same Referer rules the page would produce (REF01's levels — carry the document URL's referrer per policy, never leak the full URL of a sensitive page as referer to a third-party host); (d) PROXY/TOR HARD RULE — if an application proxy is set, the Rust engine MUST use it (reqwest Proxy) — and in a TOR WINDOW downloads must NEVER go direct: either route via the managed tor's SOCKS5 (preferred — keeps acceleration in tor mode) or fall back to the engine path entirely (acceptable v1) — but a direct clearnet fetch from a tor window is a DE-ANONYMIZATION BUG, treat as release-blocker; (e) PRIVATE-IP/SSRF — apply the same private/LAN host rules the interceptors use (a page can't use the download engine to probe intranet hosts). Verify: fixture matrix — http URL upgrades under httpsFirst, http blocked under httpsOnly, tracker-domain URL refused, redirect chain re-gated + cross-host cookie strip asserted, tor-mode download provably exits via SOCKS or engine (no direct socket), UA matches profile UA; make check PASS. | blocked | 0 | moved to rust lane — lives on branch rust-work in the arora-rust worktree (.devin/Arora-Task-Rust.md); flip done there, not here |

| SESS02 | Unclean-exit session flush — close the OS-kill gap in session persistence (user directive: session must survive 'the app crashes or is closed by the OS'). RCORE03 already made each save atomic + corruption-proof and SESS01's AutoSaver debounces saves on tab changes, but NOTHING flushes on termination signals today: SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP take the process down through the default handler — the session on disk is only as fresh as the last AutoSaver debounce fired, and a timed kill or logout can lose the in-flight change set entirely. Spec: (a) POSIX-safe signal handling — install handlers for SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP that CANNOT do Qt work in the raw handler (async-signal-safety): the standard socketpair+write-one-byte -> QSocketNotifier->event-loop bounce (or equivalent); the notifier slot calls AutoSaver::saveIfNeccessary()/saveSession() directly bypassing the debounce, then re-raises/_exits promptly — the rustcore session write is already temp+fsync+rename atomic so a final flush is always safe to issue; (b) wedged-loop cap — if the event loop can't run the flush, an alarm()/watchdog fallback exits anyway so a hung app still dies on the deadline rather than ignoring SIGTERM; (c) clean-exit distinction stays honest — normal Quit already saves; the handler only adds the termination-signal path; private windows still excluded (saveSession's isPrivate skip covers it); (d) Windows note — SetConsoleCtrlHandler/WM_CLOSE equivalent documented for the WIN01 port but POSIX-only implementation here.
